|---|---|---|
| Translating each word in source order | Target sentence becomes unnatural or unclear | Rebuild the sentence in target-language order |
| Changing a possibility into certainty | Modality changes the claim | Preserve `may`, `can`, `must`, or their equivalents |
| Dropping `if`, `unless`, or `although` | Logical relationship changes | Preserve condition or contrast |
| Adding an actor absent from a passive source | Adds an unsupported fact | Keep passive or use a neutral construction |
| Rendering an official term inconsistently | Reader may think it names two things | Choose a standard equivalent and use it consistently |
| Translating a proper name literally | The official identity may be lost | Retain or transliterate the official name |
| Changing singular/plural or tense | Timeline or quantity changes | Compare each verb and noun against the source |

## भाग 3: प्रामाणिक तथ्य, आंकड़े एवं संदर्भ

| Syllabus detail | Translation task |
|---|---|
| General Hindi, Part B | Translate an English paragraph into Hindi; approximate passage limit is 50 words |
| General English, Part B | Translate five sentences from Hindi into English |
| General Hindi total | 90 marks |
| General English total | 70 marks |

**Official source:** [RPSC Scheme & Syllabus of Examination, dated 09-01-2026](https://rpsc.rajasthan.gov.in/Static/Syllabus/238A2E0E-B0D0-49C2-8E5E-D299A915037F.pdf)

**Syllabus interpretation:** The book outline proposed translation in both directions. The 2026 syllabus specifies an English-to-Hindi paragraph in the Hindi unit and five Hindi-to-English sentences in the English unit. The model work here follows those specified directions. The original sample is not a previous examination question.

## भाग 4: स्पष्टता और तुलना: जानिए सूक्ष्म अंतर

| Translation choice | What it preserves | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Literal translation | Word order, often at the expense of meaning | Avoid when target syntax becomes unnatural |
| Meaning-based translation | Intended message and relationship | `अभिलेख संलग्न करें` → `Attach the record` |
| Transliteration | Sound/name in another script | Official scheme or place name |
| Translation of an official term | Meaning in an accepted administrative equivalent | `प्रतिवेदन` → `report` |
| Transcreation/adaptation | Recasts a message for a new audience | Not the task in a faithful exam translation |

| Source feature | Translation check |
|---|---|
| `may/can` | preserve possibility or permission |
| `must/should` | preserve obligation or recommendation |
| conditional clause | preserve the condition and outcome |
| passive construction | do not invent an actor |
| date, number, name | copy exactly and verify |
| formal request | use a courteous, appropriately formal target phrase |
| idiom | translate the intended meaning, not the literal image |

## भाग 5: उच्च-प्रतिफल बहुविकल्पीय प्रश्न (विस्तृत व्याख्या सहित)

**Question 1:** Translate correctly: `अधिकारी ने अभिलेखों की जांच की।`

A. The officer examined the records.  
B. The officer examination the records.  
C. The officer was examine records.  
D. The officer has examining the records yesterday.

**Answer: A.** It uses the correct past-tense verb and plural object.

**Question 2:** Translate correctly: `कृपया आवेदन संलग्न करें।`

A. Please attach the application.  
B. Please attached the application.  
C. Please attachment application.  
D. Please to attach application yesterday.

**Answer: A.** A polite imperative uses the base verb `attach`.

**Question 3:** Translate `यदि दस्तावेज अधूरे हैं, तो आवेदन लौटाया जा सकता है.`

A. If the documents are incomplete, the application may be returned.  
B. The documents are incomplete because the application returned.  
C. The application must never be returned.  
D. Although the documents are complete, return the application.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the condition and possibility expressed by `जा सकता है`.

**Question 4:** Translate `The order will take effect next month.`

A. आदेश अगले महीने से प्रभावी होगा।  
B. आदेश पिछले महीने प्रभावी हुआ।  
C. आदेश अगले महीने रद्द होगा।  
D. आदेश के कारण महीना प्रभावी होगा।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the future time and meaning of taking effect.

**Question 5:** Which translation preserves the passive meaning without inventing an actor? `The report was submitted after the meeting.`

A. बैठक के बाद प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया गया।  
B. अधिकारी ने बैठक से पहले प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया।  
C. समिति बैठक के बाद प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत करेगी।  
D. बैठक ने प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the passive construction and sequence without naming an unsupported actor.

**Question 6:** Translate `All departments are requested to provide the information on time.`

A. सभी विभागों से समय पर सूचना देने का अनुरोध है।  
B. सभी विभागों ने समय पर सूचना मांगी।  
C. सभी विभाग सूचना के अनुरोध हैं।  
D. समय पर विभागों को अनुरोध सूचना है।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the request, recipients, and deadline meaning.

**Question 7:** Translate `कृपया शुक्रवार से पहले प्रपत्र जमा करें।`

A. Please submit the form before Friday.  
B. Please submitted the form after Friday.  
C. The form submits Friday before you.  
D. Please submit Friday to the form.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the polite instruction and deadline.

**Question 8:** In translation, `may be returned` should preserve:

A. possibility  
B. certainty  
C. past completion  
D. prohibition

**Answer: A.** `May` indicates possibility or permission depending on context; here it expresses possibility.

**Question 9:** Translate `Keep the receipt for future reference.`

A. भविष्य में संदर्भ के लिए रसीद सुरक्षित रखें।  
B. भविष्य ने रसीद संदर्भ रखी।  
C. रसीद भविष्य को संदर्भित करती है।  
D. संदर्भ के लिए रसीद खो दें।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the instruction and purpose.

**Question 10:** Which approach is best for translating an official term?

A. Use a standard equivalent consistently.  
B. Invent a new translation in every sentence.  
C. Translate the letters one by one.  
D. Omit it automatically.

**Answer: A.** Consistent standard terminology reduces ambiguity.

**Question 11:** A source sentence contains `unless`. What must the translation preserve?

A. The condition  
B. Only the subject  
C. A new example  
D. The source's punctuation only

**Answer: A.** `Unless` introduces a condition that changes the logical meaning.

**Question 12:** Translate `Residents can send comments through the public portal.`

A. निवासी जन-पोर्टल के माध्यम से सुझाव भेज सकते हैं।  
B. निवासी पोर्टल को सुझाव भेजना चाहिए था।  
C. निवासी टिप्पणियों के माध्यम से पोर्टल हैं।  
D. जन-पोर्टल निवासी सुझाव भेज सकता है।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the subject, ability, action, and channel.

**Question 13:** Which is the most natural English translation of `विवाद का समाधान बातचीत से किया जा सकता है`?

A. The dispute can be resolved through discussion.  
B. The dispute can solution discussion.  
C. Discussion has the dispute.  
D. The dispute was discussioned.

**Answer: A.** It uses a natural passive construction and preserves possibility.

**Question 14:** When the source uses a proper scheme name, the translator should usually:

A. retain or use its standard transliteration  
B. translate each part literally  
C. replace it with a different scheme  
D. omit it

**Answer: A.** Preserving the official identity is more important than a literal rendering.

**Question 15:** Translate `The applicant may request a copy of the decision.`

A. आवेदक निर्णय की प्रति मांग सकता है।  
B. आवेदक ने निर्णय की प्रति मांगी।  
C. आवेदक निर्णय की प्रति मांगेगा ही।  
D. निर्णय आवेदक की मांग है।

**Answer: A.** It preserves possibility and the object requested.

**Question 16:** What should a translator do when the source is passive and the actor is unknown?

A. Keep a passive or neutral construction.  
B. Invent the department as actor.  
C. Change the time.  
D. Delete the action.

**Answer: A.** Adding an actor would introduce unsupported information.

**Question 17:** Translate `बैठक कल प्रातः दस बजे आयोजित होगी।`

A. The meeting will be held tomorrow at 10 a.m.  
B. The meeting was held yesterday at ten.  
C. The meeting holds the morning.  
D. The meeting may have held next week.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the future, date reference, and time.

**Question 18:** A translation changes `should` to `must`. What has changed?

A. The strength of obligation  
B. The spelling only  
C. The speaker's name  
D. The number of objects

**Answer: A.** `Must` is usually stronger than `should`; preserve the source's force.

**Question 19:** Translate `The instructions are available in both languages.`

A. निर्देश दोनों भाषाओं में उपलब्ध हैं।  
B. निर्देश दोनों भाषाओं को उपलब्ध करते हैं।  
C. दोनों भाषाएं निर्देश हैं।  
D. निर्देश एक भाषा में अनुपलब्ध थे।

**Answer: A.** It preserves availability and the two-language detail.

**Question 20:** What is the first step before translating a paragraph?

A. Read the whole paragraph and identify its main point.  
B. Translate the first word immediately.  
C. Replace every noun with a synonym.  
D. Count punctuation marks.

**Answer: A.** Understanding the whole paragraph prevents local word choices from distorting the meaning.

**Question 21:** Translate `समिति ने शिकायत की जांच करने का निर्णय लिया।`

A. The committee decided to investigate the complaint.  
B. The complaint decided to inspect the committee.  
C. The committee decision the complaint investigated.  
D. The committee has deciding the complaint yesterday.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the actor, decision, and infinitive action.

**Question 22:** Which translation preserves `because` as a reason?

A. सेवा बंद रही क्योंकि मरम्मत चल रही थी।  
B. सेवा बंद रही हालांकि मरम्मत चल रही थी।  
C. सेवा बंद रही यदि मरम्मत चल रही थी।  
D. सेवा बंद रही और मरम्मत चल रही थी, without a causal link.

**Answer: A.** `क्योंकि` directly expresses the reason.

**Question 23:** Translate `The committee will meet again after the inspection.`

A. निरीक्षण के बाद समिति की फिर बैठक होगी।  
B. समिति ने निरीक्षण से पहले बैठक की।  
C. निरीक्षण समिति की बैठक है।  
D. समिति बैठक के बाद निरीक्षण करेगी yesterday.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the future meeting and its sequence after inspection.

**Question 24:** Why is word-for-word translation risky?

A. Target-language word order and collocations may differ.  
B. Every source word is untranslatable.  
C. It always shortens the text.  
D. It removes the source meaning automatically.

**Answer: A.** Natural syntax and idiomatic combinations differ between languages.

**Question 25:** Translate `No decision was taken until the evidence was reviewed.`

A. साक्ष्यों की समीक्षा होने तक कोई निर्णय नहीं लिया गया।  
B. साक्ष्यों की समीक्षा के बाद निर्णय पहले ही लिया गया था।  
C. कोई साक्ष्य निर्णय नहीं है।  
D. समीक्षा ने निर्णय लिया।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the negative action and the time condition.

**Question 26:** What should happen to a number in translation?

A. Copy it accurately and verify it against the source.  
B. Round it without instruction.  
C. Omit every number.  
D. Replace it with a familiar figure.

**Answer: A.** Numbers are factual content and must remain accurate.

**Question 27:** Translate `योजना का लाभ पात्र परिवारों तक पहुंचना चाहिए।`

A. The scheme's benefits should reach eligible families.  
B. The plan families should benefit to arrive.  
C. The benefit has reached every family.  
D. Eligible families should scheme the benefit.

**Answer: A.** It preserves the recommendation, beneficiaries, and direction of benefit.

**Question 28:** When should an idiom be translated by meaning rather than literally?

A. When a literal image would be unnatural or misleading in the target language.  
B. Never  
C. Only in a title  
D. Whenever the source has a verb

**Answer: A.** Preserve the idiom's intended meaning, not an unsuitable word image.

**Question 29:** Translate `The officer recorded the reasons for the delay.`

A. अधिकारी ने देरी के कारण दर्ज किए।  
B. अधिकारी ने देरी को कारण बनाया।  
C. देरी ने अधिकारी को दर्ज किया।  
D. अधिकारी कारण से विलंब होगा।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the action and the object recorded.

**Question 30:** If the source says `the application may be returned`, translating it as `will be returned`:

A. changes possibility into certainty  
B. changes a noun into an adjective  
C. preserves the exact modality  
D. changes English into English only

**Answer: A.** `May` and `will` express different degrees of certainty.

**Question 31:** Translate `Residents who cannot attend should have another way to submit comments.`

A. जो निवासी उपस्थित नहीं हो सकते, उनके पास सुझाव देने का दूसरा माध्यम होना चाहिए।  
B. निवासी उपस्थित नहीं हुए क्योंकि सुझाव बंद हैं।  
C. जो सुझाव उपस्थित हैं, उन्हें निवासी करना चाहिए।  
D. निवासी दूसरा माध्यम थे।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the relative clause, recommendation, and alternative channel.

**Question 32:** A translation should preserve which source features?

A. Meaning, relationships, relevant detail, and tone  
B. Word order only  
C. Punctuation but not meaning  
D. The translator's opinion

**Answer: A.** Faithful translation carries content and communicative force.

**Question 33:** Translate `The office opened a new counter for the convenience of citizens.`

A. कार्यालय ने नागरिकों की सुविधा के लिए नया काउंटर शुरू किया।  
B. नागरिकों ने कार्यालय को सुविधा दी।  
C. काउंटर नागरिकों ने खोला।  
D. कार्यालय सुविधा का नया नागरिक है।

**Answer: A.** It preserves actor, action, object, and purpose.

**Question 34:** What should be done with an ambiguous source sentence?

A. Use context; do not silently invent a specific interpretation beyond it.  
B. Add a new fact.  
C. Omit the whole sentence.  
D. Change the subject.

**Answer: A.** Translation should preserve the source's level of specificity.

**Question 35:** Translate `The report has been sent to the competent authority.`

A. प्रतिवेदन सक्षम प्राधिकारी को भेज दिया गया है।  
B. प्राधिकारी ने प्रतिवेदन भेजा होगा, certainly.  
C. रिपोर्ट अधिकार के लिए है।  
D. रिपोर्ट सक्षम थी और भेजी जाएगी।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the completed action and recipient without inventing an actor.

**Question 36:** Which is the best approach to a proper name?

A. Preserve the official name or standard transliteration.  
B. Translate its syllables literally.  
C. Replace it with a synonym.  
D. Drop it from every sentence.

**Answer: A.** Official names identify specific entities.

**Question 37:** Translate `The order will be reviewed if new evidence is received.`

A. नया साक्ष्य मिलने पर आदेश की समीक्षा की जाएगी।  
B. नया साक्ष्य मिलने के बाद आदेश कभी नहीं देखा जाएगा।  
C. आदेश नया साक्ष्य प्राप्त करेगा।  
D. आदेश की समीक्षा हुई क्योंकि कोई साक्ष्य नहीं था।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the conditional review.

**Question 38:** Why should a translator proofread target-language agreement?

A. Correct meaning can still be obscured by wrong number or tense.  
B. Agreement is unrelated to translation.  
C. Every source noun is singular.  
D. Proofreading adds new content.

**Answer: A.** Target-language grammar must be correct as well as source-faithful.

**Question 39:** Which translation preserves a polite request?

A. Please submit the form before Friday.  
B. You submitted the form last Friday.  
C. Submit the form or else, when no threat exists in source.  
D. The form was Friday.

**Answer: A.** It keeps the courteous request and deadline.

**Question 40:** Translation of `सभी विभागों से सूचना मांगी गई` should not:

A. invent which officer requested it if the source does not say  
B. preserve the departments as recipients  
C. use a passive sentence  
D. preserve the past tense

**Answer: A.** Adding an actor would add information absent from the source.

**Question 41:** In an English-to-Hindi paragraph translation, the main paragraph point should be:

A. preserved along with its key supporting ideas  
B. replaced by a shorter unrelated thought  
C. omitted if one word is difficult  
D. expanded with personal advice

**Answer: A.** The translation should carry all essential ideas.

**Question 42:** Which is a useful final comparison question?

A. Did I add, omit, or reverse any important detail?  
B. Did I use the longest available word?  
C. Did I change the names to familiar ones?  
D. Did I remove all connectors?

**Answer: A.** A source-target comparison catches the most serious fidelity errors.

**Question 43:** Translate `The application was received on 12 July.`

A. आवेदन 12 जुलाई को प्राप्त हुआ।  
B. आवेदन 12 अगस्त को भेजा गया।  
C. आवेदन जुलाई तक प्राप्त होगा।  
D. 12 आवेदन जुलाई को थे।

**Answer: A.** It preserves the event, date, and passive sense.

**Question 44:** What is the best choice when more than one target-language rendering is natural?

A. Choose the clear one that preserves meaning and register.  
B. Assume only a word-for-word version can be correct.  
C. Add explanation until all ambiguity disappears.  
D. Copy a dictionary entry.

**Answer: A.** Translation is judged by fidelity and natural target-language expression.

**Question 45:** The current RPSC syllabus specifically asks English-unit translation of:

A. five sentences from Hindi to English  
B. a 600-word essay from Hindi to English  
C. five English sentences into Sanskrit  
D. a 300-word English passage into Hindi in the English unit

**Answer: A.** The five Hindi-to-English sentences belong to General English Part B.

## भाग 6: क्विक रिवीजन: स्मरणीय बिंदु एवं संदर्भ

### 6.1 फटाफट रिवीजन: 60 परीक्षा-उपयोगी तथ्य

1. RPSC 2026 tests translation in both language units, with different directions.
2. General Hindi translates an English paragraph into Hindi.
3. The Hindi-unit passage is approximately 50 words.
4. General English translates five sentences from Hindi into English.
5. Follow the actual paper's specific translation instructions.
6. Read the whole source before translating a paragraph.
7. Identify the main subject and action.
8. Mark object, time, cause, condition, and contrast.
9. Translate the relationship, not only individual words.
10. Rebuild word order naturally in the target language.
11. Preserve the source tense.
12. Preserve singular and plural meaning.
13. Preserve the source's degree of certainty.
14. `May` can indicate possibility or permission by context.
15. `Must` is stronger than `should` in ordinary use.
16. Keep conditional clauses such as `if` and `unless`.
17. Preserve contrast introduced by `although` or `however`.
18. Do not invent an actor absent from a passive source.
19. Use passive construction if it preserves the source emphasis.
20. Use active voice when the source names the actor and action.
21. Translate official terms consistently.
22. Check administrative terms against a reliable glossary.
23. Retain proper names or use a standard transliteration.
24. Do not translate official names literally by syllable.
25. Translate idioms by intended meaning where a literal image misleads.
26. Do not add a recommendation absent from the source.
27. Do not drop essential supporting detail from a paragraph.
28. Verify all dates and numbers against the source.
29. Keep a formal register for formal source material.
30. Use natural collocations in the target language.
31. English word order need not match Hindi word order.
32. Hindi word order need not match English word order.
33. A polite imperative often begins with `Please` in English.
34. `अभिलेख` can be translated as `record` in context.
35. `प्रतिवेदन` can be translated as `report` in context.
36. `सक्षम प्राधिकारी` is `competent authority` in administrative usage.
37. `संलग्न करें` often means `attach` in document instructions.
38. `प्रभावी होगा` can mean `will take effect`.
39. `जांच करना` can be `examine` or `investigate` depending on context.
40. `प्रार्थना/अनुरोध` requires a suitable polite register.
41. Do not translate a word by its first memorized meaning alone.
42. Read the source clause around a technical word.
43. Use connectors to preserve logic.
44. Check the target-language subject-verb agreement.
45. Check article and preposition use in English output.
46. Check gender, number, and postpositions in Hindi output.
47. Compare the final version with the source line by line.
48. Check for added information.
49. Check for omitted information.
50. Check that no cause and result have been reversed.
51. More than one translation may be natural and correct.
52. Clarity and fidelity matter more than literal word order.
53. Do not claim a practice example is a previous RPSC question.
54. Keep sentence translations concise.
55. Preserve the same names and actors across linked sentences.
56. Preserve dates, amounts, and reference numbers exactly.
57. Translate paragraph meaning as a connected whole.
58. Hindi paragraph translation should not become a summary.
59. Translation is distinct from précis writing.
60. Final check: meaning, grammar, register, and source fidelity.

### 6.2 अन्य पुस्तकों से जुड़ाव (Cross-References)

- **Chapter 2:** spelling and sentence correction help polish the translated output.
- **Chapter 3:** administrative terminology supplies consistent equivalents.
- **Chapter 7:** English grammar supports Hindi-to-English sentence accuracy.
- **Chapter 9:** comprehension and précis are separate skills from full translation.
- **Chapter 11:** formal composition uses the same clear administrative register.

**Chapter source:** RPSC, *Scheme & Syllabus of Examination*, dated 09-01-2026, Paper IV, Units I–II.

### अभ्यास प्रश्न

1. Translate correctly: `अधिकारी ने अभिलेखों की जांच की।`
   A. The officer examined the records.
   B. The officer examination the records.
   C. The officer was examine records.
   D. The officer has examining the records yesterday.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It uses the correct past-tense verb and plural object

2. Translate correctly: `कृपया आवेदन संलग्न करें।`
   A. Please attach the application.
   B. Please attached the application.
   C. Please attachment application.
   D. Please to attach application yesterday.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A polite imperative uses the base verb `attach`

3. Translate `यदि दस्तावेज अधूरे हैं, तो आवेदन लौटाया जा सकता है.`
   A. If the documents are incomplete, the application may be returned.
   B. The documents are incomplete because the application returned.
   C. The application must never be returned.
   D. Although the documents are complete, return the application.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the condition and possibility expressed by `जा सकता है`

4. Translate `The order will take effect next month.`
   A. आदेश अगले महीने से प्रभावी होगा।
   B. आदेश पिछले महीने प्रभावी हुआ।
   C. आदेश अगले महीने रद्द होगा।
   D. आदेश के कारण महीना प्रभावी होगा।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the future time and meaning of taking effect

5. Which translation preserves the passive meaning without inventing an actor? `The report was submitted after the meeting.`
   A. बैठक के बाद प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया गया।
   B. अधिकारी ने बैठक से पहले प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया।
   C. समिति बैठक के बाद प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत करेगी।
   D. बैठक ने प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the passive construction and sequence without naming an unsupported actor

6. Translate `All departments are requested to provide the information on time.`
   A. सभी विभागों से समय पर सूचना देने का अनुरोध है।
   B. सभी विभागों ने समय पर सूचना मांगी।
   C. सभी विभाग सूचना के अनुरोध हैं।
   D. समय पर विभागों को अनुरोध सूचना है।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the request, recipients, and deadline meaning

7. Translate `कृपया शुक्रवार से पहले प्रपत्र जमा करें।`
   A. Please submit the form before Friday.
   B. Please submitted the form after Friday.
   C. The form submits Friday before you.
   D. Please submit Friday to the form.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the polite instruction and deadline

8. In translation, `may be returned` should preserve:
   A. possibility
   B. certainty
   C. past completion
   D. prohibition
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `May` indicates possibility or permission depending on context; here it expresses possibility

9. Translate `Keep the receipt for future reference.`
   A. भविष्य में संदर्भ के लिए रसीद सुरक्षित रखें।
   B. भविष्य ने रसीद संदर्भ रखी।
   C. रसीद भविष्य को संदर्भित करती है।
   D. संदर्भ के लिए रसीद खो दें।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the instruction and purpose

10. Which approach is best for translating an official term?
   A. Use a standard equivalent consistently.
   B. Invent a new translation in every sentence.
   C. Translate the letters one by one.
   D. Omit it automatically.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Consistent standard terminology reduces ambiguity

11. A source sentence contains `unless`. What must the translation preserve?
   A. The condition
   B. Only the subject
   C. A new example
   D. The source's punctuation only
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `Unless` introduces a condition that changes the logical meaning

12. Translate `Residents can send comments through the public portal.`
   A. निवासी जन-पोर्टल के माध्यम से सुझाव भेज सकते हैं।
   B. निवासी पोर्टल को सुझाव भेजना चाहिए था।
   C. निवासी टिप्पणियों के माध्यम से पोर्टल हैं।
   D. जन-पोर्टल निवासी सुझाव भेज सकता है।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the subject, ability, action, and channel

13. Which is the most natural English translation of `विवाद का समाधान बातचीत से किया जा सकता है`?
   A. The dispute can be resolved through discussion.
   B. The dispute can solution discussion.
   C. Discussion has the dispute.
   D. The dispute was discussioned.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It uses a natural passive construction and preserves possibility

14. When the source uses a proper scheme name, the translator should usually:
   A. retain or use its standard transliteration
   B. translate each part literally
   C. replace it with a different scheme
   D. omit it
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Preserving the official identity is more important than a literal rendering

15. Translate `The applicant may request a copy of the decision.`
   A. आवेदक निर्णय की प्रति मांग सकता है।
   B. आवेदक ने निर्णय की प्रति मांगी।
   C. आवेदक निर्णय की प्रति मांगेगा ही।
   D. निर्णय आवेदक की मांग है।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves possibility and the object requested

16. What should a translator do when the source is passive and the actor is unknown?
   A. Keep a passive or neutral construction.
   B. Invent the department as actor.
   C. Change the time.
   D. Delete the action.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Adding an actor would introduce unsupported information

17. Translate `बैठक कल प्रातः दस बजे आयोजित होगी।`
   A. The meeting will be held tomorrow at 10 a.m.
   B. The meeting was held yesterday at ten.
   C. The meeting holds the morning.
   D. The meeting may have held next week.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the future, date reference, and time

18. A translation changes `should` to `must`. What has changed?
   A. The strength of obligation
   B. The spelling only
   C. The speaker's name
   D. The number of objects
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `Must` is usually stronger than `should`; preserve the source's force

19. Translate `The instructions are available in both languages.`
   A. निर्देश दोनों भाषाओं में उपलब्ध हैं।
   B. निर्देश दोनों भाषाओं को उपलब्ध करते हैं।
   C. दोनों भाषाएं निर्देश हैं।
   D. निर्देश एक भाषा में अनुपलब्ध थे।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves availability and the two-language detail

20. What is the first step before translating a paragraph?
   A. Read the whole paragraph and identify its main point.
   B. Translate the first word immediately.
   C. Replace every noun with a synonym.
   D. Count punctuation marks.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Understanding the whole paragraph prevents local word choices from distorting the meaning

21. Translate `समिति ने शिकायत की जांच करने का निर्णय लिया।`
   A. The committee decided to investigate the complaint.
   B. The complaint decided to inspect the committee.
   C. The committee decision the complaint investigated.
   D. The committee has deciding the complaint yesterday.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the actor, decision, and infinitive action

22. Which translation preserves `because` as a reason?
   A. सेवा बंद रही क्योंकि मरम्मत चल रही थी।
   B. सेवा बंद रही हालांकि मरम्मत चल रही थी।
   C. सेवा बंद रही यदि मरम्मत चल रही थी।
   D. सेवा बंद रही और मरम्मत चल रही थी, without a causal link.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `क्योंकि` directly expresses the reason

23. Translate `The committee will meet again after the inspection.`
   A. निरीक्षण के बाद समिति की फिर बैठक होगी।
   B. समिति ने निरीक्षण से पहले बैठक की।
   C. निरीक्षण समिति की बैठक है।
   D. समिति बैठक के बाद निरीक्षण करेगी yesterday.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the future meeting and its sequence after inspection

24. Why is word-for-word translation risky?
   A. Target-language word order and collocations may differ.
   B. Every source word is untranslatable.
   C. It always shortens the text.
   D. It removes the source meaning automatically.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Natural syntax and idiomatic combinations differ between languages

25. Translate `No decision was taken until the evidence was reviewed.`
   A. साक्ष्यों की समीक्षा होने तक कोई निर्णय नहीं लिया गया।
   B. साक्ष्यों की समीक्षा के बाद निर्णय पहले ही लिया गया था।
   C. कोई साक्ष्य निर्णय नहीं है।
   D. समीक्षा ने निर्णय लिया।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the negative action and the time condition

26. What should happen to a number in translation?
   A. Copy it accurately and verify it against the source.
   B. Round it without instruction.
   C. Omit every number.
   D. Replace it with a familiar figure.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Numbers are factual content and must remain accurate

27. Translate `योजना का लाभ पात्र परिवारों तक पहुंचना चाहिए।`
   A. The scheme's benefits should reach eligible families.
   B. The plan families should benefit to arrive.
   C. The benefit has reached every family.
   D. Eligible families should scheme the benefit.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the recommendation, beneficiaries, and direction of benefit

28. When should an idiom be translated by meaning rather than literally?
   A. When a literal image would be unnatural or misleading in the target language.
   B. Never
   C. Only in a title
   D. Whenever the source has a verb
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Preserve the idiom's intended meaning, not an unsuitable word image

29. Translate `The officer recorded the reasons for the delay.`
   A. अधिकारी ने देरी के कारण दर्ज किए।
   B. अधिकारी ने देरी को कारण बनाया।
   C. देरी ने अधिकारी को दर्ज किया।
   D. अधिकारी कारण से विलंब होगा।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the action and the object recorded

30. If the source says `the application may be returned`, translating it as `will be returned`:
   A. changes possibility into certainty
   B. changes a noun into an adjective
   C. preserves the exact modality
   D. changes English into English only
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `May` and `will` express different degrees of certainty

31. Translate `Residents who cannot attend should have another way to submit comments.`
   A. जो निवासी उपस्थित नहीं हो सकते, उनके पास सुझाव देने का दूसरा माध्यम होना चाहिए।
   B. निवासी उपस्थित नहीं हुए क्योंकि सुझाव बंद हैं।
   C. जो सुझाव उपस्थित हैं, उन्हें निवासी करना चाहिए।
   D. निवासी दूसरा माध्यम थे।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the relative clause, recommendation, and alternative channel

32. A translation should preserve which source features?
   A. Meaning, relationships, relevant detail, and tone
   B. Word order only
   C. Punctuation but not meaning
   D. The translator's opinion
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Faithful translation carries content and communicative force

33. Translate `The office opened a new counter for the convenience of citizens.`
   A. कार्यालय ने नागरिकों की सुविधा के लिए नया काउंटर शुरू किया।
   B. नागरिकों ने कार्यालय को सुविधा दी।
   C. काउंटर नागरिकों ने खोला।
   D. कार्यालय सुविधा का नया नागरिक है।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves actor, action, object, and purpose

34. What should be done with an ambiguous source sentence?
   A. Use context; do not silently invent a specific interpretation beyond it.
   B. Add a new fact.
   C. Omit the whole sentence.
   D. Change the subject.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Translation should preserve the source's level of specificity

35. Translate `The report has been sent to the competent authority.`
   A. प्रतिवेदन सक्षम प्राधिकारी को भेज दिया गया है।
   B. प्राधिकारी ने प्रतिवेदन भेजा होगा, certainly.
   C. रिपोर्ट अधिकार के लिए है।
   D. रिपोर्ट सक्षम थी और भेजी जाएगी।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the completed action and recipient without inventing an actor

36. Which is the best approach to a proper name?
   A. Preserve the official name or standard transliteration.
   B. Translate its syllables literally.
   C. Replace it with a synonym.
   D. Drop it from every sentence.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Official names identify specific entities

37. Translate `The order will be reviewed if new evidence is received.`
   A. नया साक्ष्य मिलने पर आदेश की समीक्षा की जाएगी।
   B. नया साक्ष्य मिलने के बाद आदेश कभी नहीं देखा जाएगा।
   C. आदेश नया साक्ष्य प्राप्त करेगा।
   D. आदेश की समीक्षा हुई क्योंकि कोई साक्ष्य नहीं था।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the conditional review

38. Why should a translator proofread target-language agreement?
   A. Correct meaning can still be obscured by wrong number or tense.
   B. Agreement is unrelated to translation.
   C. Every source noun is singular.
   D. Proofreading adds new content.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Target-language grammar must be correct as well as source-faithful

39. Which translation preserves a polite request?
   A. Please submit the form before Friday.
   B. You submitted the form last Friday.
   C. Submit the form or else, when no threat exists in source.
   D. The form was Friday.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It keeps the courteous request and deadline

40. Translation of `सभी विभागों से सूचना मांगी गई` should not:
   A. invent which officer requested it if the source does not say
   B. preserve the departments as recipients
   C. use a passive sentence
   D. preserve the past tense
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Adding an actor would add information absent from the source

41. In an English-to-Hindi paragraph translation, the main paragraph point should be:
   A. preserved along with its key supporting ideas
   B. replaced by a shorter unrelated thought
   C. omitted if one word is difficult
   D. expanded with personal advice
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The translation should carry all essential ideas

42. Which is a useful final comparison question?
   A. Did I add, omit, or reverse any important detail?
   B. Did I use the longest available word?
   C. Did I change the names to familiar ones?
   D. Did I remove all connectors?
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A source-target comparison catches the most serious fidelity errors

43. Translate `The application was received on 12 July.`
   A. आवेदन 12 जुलाई को प्राप्त हुआ।
   B. आवेदन 12 अगस्त को भेजा गया।
   C. आवेदन जुलाई तक प्राप्त होगा।
   D. 12 आवेदन जुलाई को थे।
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It preserves the event, date, and passive sense

44. What is the best choice when more than one target-language rendering is natural?
   A. Choose the clear one that preserves meaning and register.
   B. Assume only a word-for-word version can be correct.
   C. Add explanation until all ambiguity disappears.
   D. Copy a dictionary entry.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Translation is judged by fidelity and natural target-language expression

45. The current RPSC syllabus specifically asks English-unit translation of:
   A. five sentences from Hindi to English
   B. a 600-word essay from Hindi to English
   C. five English sentences into Sanskrit
   D. a 300-word English passage into Hindi in the English unit
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The five Hindi-to-English sentences belong to General English Part B ## भाग 6: क्विक रिवीजन: स्मरणीय बिंदु एवं संदर्भ ### 6.1 फटाफट रिवीजन: 60 परीक्षा-उपयोगी तथ्य 1. RPSC 2026 tests translation in both language units, with different directions. 2. General Hindi translates an English paragraph into Hindi. 3. The Hindi-unit passage is approximately 50 words. 4. General English translates five sentences from Hindi into English. 5. Follow the actual paper's specific translation instructions. 6. Read the whole source before translating a paragraph. 7. Identify the main subject and action. 8. Mark object, time, cause, condition, and contrast. 9. Translate the relationship, not only individual words. 10. Rebuild word order naturally in the target language. 11. Preserve the source tense. 12. Preserve singular and plural meaning. 13. Preserve the source's degree of certainty. 14. `May` can indicate possibility or permission by context. 15. `Must` is stronger than `should` in ordinary use. 16. Keep conditional clauses such as `if` and `unless`. 17. Preserve contrast introduced by `although` or `however`. 18. Do not invent an actor absent from a passive source. 19. Use passive construction if it preserves the source emphasis. 20. Use active voice when the source names the actor and action. 21. Translate official terms consistently. 22. Check administrative terms against a reliable glossary. 23. Retain proper names or use a standard transliteration. 24. Do not translate official names literally by syllable. 25. Translate idioms by intended meaning where a literal image misleads. 26. Do not add a recommendation absent from the source. 27. Do not drop essential supporting detail from a paragraph. 28. Verify all dates and numbers against the source. 29. Keep a formal register for formal source material. 30. Use natural collocations in the target language. 31. English word order need not match Hindi word order. 32. Hindi word order need not match English word order. 33. A polite imperative often begins with `Please` in English. 34. `अभिलेख` can be translated as `record` in context. 35. `प्रतिवेदन` can be translated as `report` in context. 36. `सक्षम प्राधिकारी` is `competent authority` in administrative usage. 37. `संलग्न करें` often means `attach` in document instructions. 38. `प्रभावी होगा` can mean `will take effect`. 39. `जांच करना` can be `examine` or `investigate` depending on context. 40. `प्रार्थना/अनुरोध` requires a suitable polite register. 41. Do not translate a word by its first memorized meaning alone. 42. Read the source clause around a technical word. 43. Use connectors to preserve logic. 44. Check the target-language subject-verb agreement. 45. Check article and preposition use in English output. 46. Check gender, number, and postpositions in Hindi output. 47. Compare the final version with the source line by line. 48. Check for added information. 49. Check for omitted information. 50. Check that no cause and result have been reversed. 51. More than one translation may be natural and correct. 52. Clarity and fidelity matter more than literal word order. 53. Do not claim a practice example is a previous RPSC question. 54. Keep sentence translations concise. 55. Preserve the same names and actors across linked sentences. 56. Preserve dates, amounts, and reference numbers exactly. 57. Translate paragraph meaning as a connected whole. 58. Hindi paragraph translation should not become a summary. 59. Translation is distinct from précis writing. 60. Final check: meaning, grammar, register, and source fidelity. ### 6.2 अन्य पुस्तकों से जुड़ाव (Cross-References) - **Chapter 2:** spelling and sentence correction help polish the translated output. - **Chapter 3:** administrative terminology supplies consistent equivalents. - **Chapter 7:** English grammar supports Hindi-to-English sentence accuracy. - **Chapter 9:** comprehension and précis are separate skills from full translation. - **Chapter 11:** formal composition uses the same clear administrative register. --- Chapter source:** RPSC, *Scheme & Syllabus of Examination*, dated 09-01-2026, Paper IV, Units I–II.

## अध्याय 11: English Composition: Letters, Reports and Focused Paragraphs

## भाग 1: अध्याय प्रवेश: परीक्षा दृष्टिकोण एवं विगत प्रश्न

The RPSC Paper IV syllabus dated 9 January 2026 gives 20 marks to General English Composition & Letter Writing. It asks candidates to elaborate one of three given themes in approximately 150 words, and to write an official letter, demi-official letter, official memorandum, or report in approximately 150 words. The actual question paper determines the selected format and any special instructions.

Strong composition has one clear purpose, an appropriate structure, accurate details, and language that suits the reader. A format is a working guide; it cannot replace facts, authority, or the conditions in a question.

## भाग 2: संकल्पना विस्तार: Purposeful English Composition

### 2.1 Theme elaboration in approximately 150 words

When asked to elaborate a theme, do not turn a short prompt into a full-length essay. A compact structure works well:

1. **Opening:** state the central idea in one or two sentences.
2. **Development:** explain two or three connected aspects, such as cause, effect, benefit, risk, or responsibility.
3. **Example or application:** give one relevant, verifiable or clearly general example.
4. **Closing:** return to the theme and state a balanced implication.

**Practice theme:** “Good public information prevents avoidable delay.”

**Model elaboration — approximately 150 words**

Clear public information is a practical part of good administration. When citizens know which documents are required, where to apply, what a service costs, and when a response is due, they can approach an office prepared. This reduces repeat visits and helps staff spend more time resolving cases. Information must also be understandable and current. A notice filled with technical language may meet a formal requirement but still fail the reader. Offices should use plain wording, display contact details, and update instructions whenever a procedure changes. Digital information can reach many people quickly, but a help desk or assisted option remains important for citizens who cannot easily use an online service. Clear information does not remove every delay, because some cases require verification or additional evidence. It does, however, prevent confusion that the office can control. Public communication is therefore not an extra courtesy; it is part of reliable, fair, and timely service.

The model focuses on one theme, gives practical consequences, acknowledges a limitation, and ends with a relevant conclusion. It does not depend on invented figures.

### 2.2 Official letter

**Purpose:** communicate a specific request, response, or official matter to a named recipient.

**Useful elements:** issuing office; reference and date; recipient's designation/address; subject; reference to earlier communication where relevant; concise body; requested action and time; courteous close; signature, name, and designation; enclosures or copy list where necessary.

**Model opening and body**

Office of the Sub-Divisional Officer  
Example Sub-Division  
Reference: 18/Service/2026  
15 September 2026

To  
The Chief Executive Officer  
Example Panchayat Samiti

**Subject: Submission of updated service-camp progress**

Sir/Madam,

Please provide an updated statement of applications received and cases resolved at the service camps by 20 September 2026. Use the attached format and have the statement verified by the designated officer. This information is required to plan follow-up assistance for pending cases.

Yours faithfully,  
Signature  
Name and designation

This is a fictional study example. Keep names, dates, reference numbers, and authorities exactly as supplied in an examination question.

### 2.3 Demi-official letter and official memorandum

A **demi-official letter** is an official communication between officers that invites personal attention to an official matter. The tone can be more personal than a standard official letter, but the subject, requested action, and record remain clear.

**Model opening:**

Dear Ms Sharma,

I am writing to request your personal attention to the pending service-camp statements. The district review is scheduled for 24 September. Please arrange for the verified figures to reach this office by 20 September so that unresolved applications can be identified in advance.

An **official memorandum** communicates an official matter in the prescribed office form. It is usually organized around the issuing office, number/date, subject, the communication or decision, any effective date, and distribution. Do not assume that a central format automatically governs every state office; follow the exam prompt or the relevant department's form.

### 2.4 Report writing

A short report may use the following sequence:

| Section | Include |
|---|---|
| Title / subject | The event, inspection, or issue covered |
| Purpose and period | Why the report was prepared and dates covered |
| Method / sources | Records, observations, or interviews used |
| Findings | Verified facts, in a logical order |
| Conclusion | What the findings support |
| Recommendation | Practical next action, clearly marked as a recommendation |
| Date and author | Report writer's name/designation where requested |

Distinguish observation from allegation, inference, and recommendation. If evidence is incomplete, say so. Never report a claim as a proven fact merely because it appears in a complaint.

**Model report — fictional practice**

**Report on the Public Service Help Desk, Example Block**

The help desk was reviewed on 12 September 2026 to assess access to application guidance. The review covered the displayed notice, staff availability, and the method for referring unresolved questions. The notice listed the service hours and contact point, but it did not identify the documents required for two common applications. During the visit, staff were available and directed visitors to the relevant counters. Three visitors asked the same question about supporting records. These observations suggest that the notice should be updated with a checklist and reviewed after procedural changes. The block office should display the revised notice in clear language and check, after one month, whether repeat queries have declined. This report records observations from one visit and should not be treated as a full audit of the service.

The final sentence limits the claim to the evidence collected. That makes the report more credible, not less useful.

### 2.5 Coherence and word control

Use topic sentences to show the point of each paragraph. Connect ideas with accurate transitions such as `however`, `therefore`, `for example`, and `in addition`. Do not use a connector merely for decoration. Vary sentence length, but keep one main point in each sentence where possible.

For approximately 150 words, aim for three or four compact paragraphs, or one organized paragraph if the task is a theme elaboration. Leave a few minutes to check the word limit, names, tense, agreement, and whether the requested format is complete.

## भाग 3: प्रामाणिक तथ्य, आंकड़े एवं संदर्भ

| Official syllabus item | Requirement |
|---|---|
| General English: 70 marks | Unit total |
| Composition & Letter Writing: 20 marks | Theme elaboration and official writing |
| Theme elaboration | Choose one of three themes; approximately 150 words |
| Official writing | Official letter, demi-official letter, official memorandum, or report writing; approximately 150 words |

**Official source:** [RPSC Scheme & Syllabus of Examination, dated 09-01-2026](https://rpsc.rajasthan.gov.in/Static/Syllabus/238A2E0E-B0D0-49C2-8E5E-D299A915037F.pdf)

The syllabus specifies approximate length and task types. It does not prescribe the exact scoring rubric or guarantee a fixed format in every paper. The sample names, dates, and offices in this chapter are fictional.

## भाग 4: स्पष्टता और तुलना: जानिए सूक्ष्म अंतर

| Task | Main purpose | Typical structure |
|---|---|---|
| Theme elaboration | Develop a given idea briefly | Central idea → explanation → application → conclusion |
| Official letter | Communicate with a named recipient | Reference/date → recipient → subject → body → action → signature |
| Demi-official letter | Bring personal attention to an official issue | Officer-to-officer, direct tone, clear request |
| Official memorandum | Record or communicate an office matter | Number/date → subject → decision or information → distribution |
| Report | Present findings and next steps | Purpose → method → verified findings → conclusion → recommendation |

| Weak choice | Better choice |
|---|---|
| State an allegation as a confirmed fact | Identify it as a complaint or unverified claim |
| Add a statistic from memory | Use a verified fact or a general example |
| Write a long introduction before the point | State the purpose in the opening lines |
| Omit the requested action | Name who should do what and by when |
| Use `therefore` without a logical result | Choose a connector that matches the relationship |
| Reuse a generic letter format for a report | Select the structure that fits the task |

## भाग 5: उच्च-प्रतिफल बहुविकल्पीय प्रश्न (विस्तृत व्याख्या सहित)

**Question 1:** The official syllabus gives approximately how many words for theme elaboration?

A. 50  
B. 100  
C. 150  
D. 600

**Answer: C.** The 2026 syllabus specifies approximately 150 words.

**Question 2:** How many theme options does the syllabus say candidates choose from?

A. One  
B. Two  
C. Three  
D. Six

**Answer: C.** Candidates elaborate any one of three themes.

**Question 3:** The listed official-writing formats include:

A. official letter, demi-official letter, official memorandum, and report  
B. poem, story, and dialogue only  
C. press release only  
D. précis only

**Answer: A.** Those four formats appear in the current syllabus.

**Question 4:** What is the main purpose of a subject line?

A. Identify the matter concisely  
B. Replace the letter body  
C. Add an unrelated slogan  
D. List all attachments in detail

**Answer: A.** A subject line lets the recipient identify the issue quickly.

**Question 5:** A useful official letter body should state:

A. relevant facts and the requested action  
B. only a greeting  
C. every background detail  
D. an unverified accusation

**Answer: A.** The recipient needs the issue and the action expected.

**Question 6:** A demi-official letter is generally used to:

A. invite an officer's personal attention to an official matter  
B. replace every formal record  
C. issue a public legal notice  
D. publish a news story

**Answer: A.** It adds a personal channel between officers while remaining official.

**Question 7:** Which is essential in a short report?

A. Verified findings  
B. Personal attack  
C. Unsupported statistics  
D. Decorative idioms

**Answer: A.** A report must distinguish evidence from opinion.

**Question 8:** A recommendation in a report should be:

A. clearly labelled and connected to findings  
B. presented as an observed fact  
C. unrelated to the issue  
D. omitted in every report

**Answer: A.** Readers should know which statements are findings and which propose action.

**Question 9:** If information is incomplete, a report writer should:

A. state the limitation  
B. invent the missing details  
C. report an allegation as proven  
D. conceal the method

**Answer: A.** A clear limitation keeps the conclusion proportionate to the evidence.

**Question 10:** Which opening best suits a 150-word theme elaboration?

A. A direct statement of the central idea  
B. A long unrelated story  
C. A list of dictionary definitions  
D. A quotation with an unverified author

**Answer: A.** A direct opening leaves room to explain and conclude the idea.

**Question 11:** What does a topic sentence do?

A. Signals the paragraph's main point  
B. Adds a reference number  
C. Names the recipient  
D. Replaces all evidence

**Answer: A.** It helps the reader follow the paragraph's purpose.

**Question 12:** Which connector introduces contrast?

A. however  
B. therefore  
C. for example  
D. first

**Answer: A.** `However` commonly signals contrast or qualification.

**Question 13:** Which connector introduces a result?

A. therefore  
B. although  
C. for instance  
D. meanwhile

**Answer: A.** `Therefore` signals a conclusion or consequence.

**Question 14:** What should the closing of a short theme elaboration do?

A. Return to the theme and state its implication  
B. Start a new subject  
C. Repeat the opening word-for-word  
D. Add an unrelated statistic

**Answer: A.** A concise closing completes the argument.

**Question 15:** A report on a single site visit should:

A. limit conclusions to what the visit supports  
B. claim a complete audit of every service  
C. omit the date  
D. assume all offices are identical

**Answer: A.** Scope and evidence must align.

**Question 16:** Why include a method or source section in a report?

A. It shows how findings were obtained.  
B. It replaces findings.  
C. It hides the evidence.  
D. It supplies a greeting.

**Answer: A.** The method helps readers assess the basis of the report.

**Question 17:** The phrase `the notice was difficult to read` is best classified as:

A. an observation or finding if supported by the review  
B. a recommendation  
C. a reference number  
D. a subject line

**Answer: A.** It describes an observed condition; recommendations follow separately.

**Question 18:** Which phrase is a recommendation?

A. The office should display a revised checklist.  
B. The notice listed service hours.  
C. Three visitors asked a question.  
D. The review took place on 12 September.

**Answer: A.** `Should display` proposes an action.

**Question 19:** Which detail is most useful in a reference line?

A. A prior letter's number and date  
B. A general slogan  
C. An unrelated name  
D. A personal opinion

**Answer: A.** The reference identifies earlier correspondence.

**Question 20:** What should an office letter specify when action is time-sensitive?

A. A reasonable deadline  
B. No timeframe  
C. Only the sender's view  
D. An unrelated date

**Answer: A.** A clear deadline supports timely action.

**Question 21:** The model practice offices and dates in this chapter are:

A. fictional examples  
B. verified government orders  
C. official RPSC templates  
D. legal notifications

**Answer: A.** They are labelled as fictional practice material.

**Question 22:** In an examination task, candidate-supplied names and dates should:

A. follow the prompt exactly  
B. be replaced with real officials' names  
C. be guessed  
D. be omitted automatically

**Answer: A.** Do not alter facts supplied in the question.

**Question 23:** Which statement is appropriate when a complaint has not been verified?

A. The complaint alleges that the service was delayed.  
B. The officer is proven guilty.  
C. The event definitely occurred.  
D. The report should invent a finding.

**Answer: A.** It reports the claim as a claim, not as a confirmed conclusion.

**Question 24:** Which format best presents inspection findings and suggestions?

A. Report  
B. Agenda  
C. Personal invitation  
D. Phrasal-verb list

**Answer: A.** A report organizes method, findings, conclusion, and recommendation.

**Question 25:** Which format is designed to communicate with a named recipient on a specific official matter?

A. Official letter  
B. Glossary  
C. Minutes  
D. Theme elaboration

**Answer: A.** An official letter addresses a particular recipient and matter.

**Question 26:** Which form is commonly a structured internal office communication or decision note?

A. Official memorandum  
B. Essay  
C. Idiom  
D. Comprehension passage

**Answer: A.** An official memorandum communicates an office matter in the prescribed form.

**Question 27:** A demi-official letter's tone may be more personal, but it should still:

A. state the official subject and requested action clearly  
B. omit all dates  
C. make unsupported claims  
D. abandon the record

**Answer: A.** Personal tone does not remove the need for clarity.

**Question 28:** What is the best way to stay near a 150-word limit?

A. Plan a short structure and remove repetition.  
B. Write without checking.  
C. Add a second topic.  
D. Copy a long quotation.

**Answer: A.** Planning and editing keep the response focused.

**Question 29:** Why should a report distinguish facts from recommendations?

A. Readers need to know what was observed and what action is proposed.  
B. Both are always identical.  
C. Recommendations are legal findings.  
D. Facts should be omitted.

**Answer: A.** Clear labels prevent a proposed action from being mistaken for evidence.

**Question 30:** What should a writer do with an unsupported statistic?

A. Omit it or verify it before use.  
B. Include it for emphasis.  
C. Round it to a larger number.  
D. Attribute it to an unnamed official.

**Answer: A.** Unsupported data reduces reliability.

**Question 31:** Which sentence is most concise?

A. The office will review the application.  
B. A review of the application will be undertaken by the office.  
C. The application, which is the subject, will be subject to a review.  
D. The office will make a review that reviews the application.

**Answer: A.** It expresses the action directly with fewer words.

**Question 32:** The word limit applies to:

A. the response, as directed by the question  
B. only the address  
C. only the title in every task  
D. nothing in the paper

**Answer: A.** Follow the specific instructions about what to include in the count.

**Question 33:** What should a writer check in the final minute?

A. Names, dates, action, agreement, and format  
B. Only handwriting size  
C. Only the first sentence  
D. Whether a new statistic can be added

**Answer: A.** These checks catch content and language errors.

**Question 34:** Which sentence is appropriately formal?

A. Please provide the verified statement by 20 September.  
B. Send it whenever you get around to it, okay?  
C. Yo, forward the figures.  
D. Do the thing soon.

**Answer: A.** It is courteous, clear, and specifies a deadline.

**Question 35:** A report's conclusion should:

A. follow from the findings  
B. contradict them without explanation  
C. add a new allegation  
D. introduce an unrelated theme

**Answer: A.** Conclusions should be evidence-based.

**Question 36:** Which is the clearest request?

A. Please send the verified list by 20 September.  
B. The list is involved with movement.  
C. Kindly consider all things appropriately.  
D. A list might happen.

**Answer: A.** It names the action, object, and time.

**Question 37:** What is the role of a distribution/copy list?

A. Identify offices that receive a copy  
B. Replace the subject  
C. Prove the report's findings  
D. Set the word limit

**Answer: A.** It records who else receives the communication.

**Question 38:** What does `in addition` signal?

A. another related point  
B. a direct contradiction  
C. a condition  
D. a final deadline

**Answer: A.** It adds a related point.

**Question 39:** What is the main risk of overusing formal passive constructions?

A. The responsible actor may become unclear.  
B. The sentence becomes a question.  
C. The date becomes invalid.  
D. The word count becomes zero.

**Answer: A.** Passive voice can hide who is expected to act.

**Question 40:** What is the best way to handle a limitation in a report?

A. State it plainly and keep conclusions within the evidence.  
B. Hide it.  
C. Invent a broader sample.  
D. Delete all findings.

**Answer: A.** Transparent scope improves trust in the report.

**Question 41:** The current syllabus word count for official writing is approximately:

A. 50  
B. 100  
C. 150  
D. 300

**Answer: C.** The syllabus indicates approximately 150 words.

**Question 42:** A theme elaboration should:

A. develop the given theme rather than introduce a separate essay topic  
B. copy the prompt repeatedly  
C. include an unrelated report format  
D. avoid a conclusion

**Answer: A.** The response should remain focused on the assigned theme.

**Question 43:** Which is a useful transition from evidence to a recommendation?

A. Therefore  
B. For example (in every context)  
C. Although  
D. Meanwhile

**Answer: A.** `Therefore` can introduce a conclusion that follows from evidence.

**Question 44:** A good report's findings should be:

A. ordered and specific  
B. mixed with unrelated opinion  
C. anonymous and undated in every case  
D. written as a story with invented dialogue

**Answer: A.** Ordered, specific findings are easier to assess.

**Question 45:** Which is the strongest final review question for a composition task?

A. Does every sentence help answer the prompt?  
B. Have I added as many idioms as possible?  
C. Is the opening longer than the body?  
D. Did I include a new fact from memory?

**Answer: A.** Relevance keeps the response focused and concise.

## भाग 6: क्विक रिवीजन: स्मरणीय बिंदु एवं संदर्भ

### 6.1 फटाफट रिवीजन: 60 परीक्षा-उपयोगी तथ्य

1. RPSC 2026 General English carries 70 marks.
2. Composition & Letter Writing carries 20 marks.
3. Theme elaboration is approximately 150 words.
4. Candidates choose one of three given themes.
5. Official writing is approximately 150 words.
6. The syllabus lists official, demi-official, memorandum, and report formats.
7. The actual paper determines the exact task.
8. A theme elaboration develops one central idea.
9. A short opening should directly state the theme.
10. Two or three connected aspects can develop the idea.
11. One relevant example may support the theme.
12. Avoid unsupported statistics.
13. A conclusion should return to the assigned theme.
14. An official letter addresses a named recipient.
15. A subject line identifies the matter.
16. A reference line can identify earlier correspondence.
17. The body should state facts and requested action.
18. A time-sensitive request should give a reasonable deadline.
19. Sign with the required name and designation.
20. Mention relevant enclosures or copies.
21. A demi-official letter seeks an officer's personal attention.
22. Demi-official tone can be more personal but remains official.
23. An official memorandum communicates an office matter in prescribed form.
24. Departmental rules may set the memorandum template.
25. A report states purpose and period.
26. A report may describe the method or sources used.
27. Findings should be factual and ordered.
28. Separate observations from allegations.
29. Separate conclusions from recommendations.
30. Recommendations should follow the findings.
31. State evidentiary limitations plainly.
32. Do not overgeneralize from a single visit.
33. Use connectors according to logical meaning.
34. `However` signals contrast or qualification.
35. `Therefore` can signal a supported result.
36. `For example` introduces an illustration.
37. A topic sentence identifies a paragraph's point.
38. One main idea per sentence often improves clarity.
39. Avoid repeating the prompt as filler.
40. Prefer direct verbs when they clarify responsibility.
41. Passive voice is useful when the actor is unknown or secondary.
42. Passive voice can hide responsibility if overused.
43. Use courteous formal wording for official requests.
44. Keep names and dates exactly as the prompt gives them.
45. A fictional model is not an official government template.
46. A central format does not automatically govern every state office.
47. The word limit is approximate; check the exam instruction.
48. Plan before writing to avoid exceeding the limit.
49. Remove repetitive background information.
50. Use plain language for public communication.
51. A report should identify its scope.
52. An allegation should not be reported as proven without evidence.
53. A theme elaboration is shorter than a full essay.
54. The RPSC essay task is approximately 600 words, covered in Chapter 12.
55. An official letter and a report serve different purposes.
56. A memorandum and a demi-official letter have different communication styles.
57. The copy list identifies additional recipients.
58. Proofread tense, subject-verb agreement, and punctuation.
59. Check that the requested action is explicit.
60. Final check: prompt, format, facts, word limit, and clarity.

### 6.2 अन्य पुस्तकों से जुड़ाव (Cross-References)

- **Chapter 6:** Hindi-language office formats cover the same administrative document family.
- **Chapter 7:** grammar and sentence editing improve English composition.
- **Chapter 8:** choose formal verbs in official writing and use idioms sparingly.
- **Chapter 9:** précis condenses a source; composition develops a prompt.
- **Chapter 12:** essay writing expands a broad theme to approximately 600 words.

**Chapter source:** RPSC, *Scheme & Syllabus of Examination*, dated 09-01-2026, Paper IV, Unit II.

### अभ्यास प्रश्न

1. The official syllabus gives approximately how many words for theme elaboration?
   A. 50
   B. 100
   C. 150
   D. 600
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** The 2026 syllabus specifies approximately 150 words

2. How many theme options does the syllabus say candidates choose from?
   A. One
   B. Two
   C. Three
   D. Six
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Candidates elaborate any one of three themes

3. The listed official-writing formats include:
   A. official letter, demi-official letter, official memorandum, and report
   B. poem, story, and dialogue only
   C. press release only
   D. précis only
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Those four formats appear in the current syllabus

4. What is the main purpose of a subject line?
   A. Identify the matter concisely
   B. Replace the letter body
   C. Add an unrelated slogan
   D. List all attachments in detail
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A subject line lets the recipient identify the issue quickly

5. A useful official letter body should state:
   A. relevant facts and the requested action
   B. only a greeting
   C. every background detail
   D. an unverified accusation
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The recipient needs the issue and the action expected

6. A demi-official letter is generally used to:
   A. invite an officer's personal attention to an official matter
   B. replace every formal record
   C. issue a public legal notice
   D. publish a news story
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It adds a personal channel between officers while remaining official

7. Which is essential in a short report?
   A. Verified findings
   B. Personal attack
   C. Unsupported statistics
   D. Decorative idioms
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A report must distinguish evidence from opinion

8. A recommendation in a report should be:
   A. clearly labelled and connected to findings
   B. presented as an observed fact
   C. unrelated to the issue
   D. omitted in every report
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Readers should know which statements are findings and which propose action

9. If information is incomplete, a report writer should:
   A. state the limitation
   B. invent the missing details
   C. report an allegation as proven
   D. conceal the method
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A clear limitation keeps the conclusion proportionate to the evidence

10. Which opening best suits a 150-word theme elaboration?
   A. A direct statement of the central idea
   B. A long unrelated story
   C. A list of dictionary definitions
   D. A quotation with an unverified author
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A direct opening leaves room to explain and conclude the idea

11. What does a topic sentence do?
   A. Signals the paragraph's main point
   B. Adds a reference number
   C. Names the recipient
   D. Replaces all evidence
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It helps the reader follow the paragraph's purpose

12. Which connector introduces contrast?
   A. however
   B. therefore
   C. for example
   D. first
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `However` commonly signals contrast or qualification

13. Which connector introduces a result?
   A. therefore
   B. although
   C. for instance
   D. meanwhile
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `Therefore` signals a conclusion or consequence

14. What should the closing of a short theme elaboration do?
   A. Return to the theme and state its implication
   B. Start a new subject
   C. Repeat the opening word-for-word
   D. Add an unrelated statistic
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A concise closing completes the argument

15. A report on a single site visit should:
   A. limit conclusions to what the visit supports
   B. claim a complete audit of every service
   C. omit the date
   D. assume all offices are identical
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Scope and evidence must align

16. Why include a method or source section in a report?
   A. It shows how findings were obtained.
   B. It replaces findings.
   C. It hides the evidence.
   D. It supplies a greeting.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The method helps readers assess the basis of the report

17. The phrase `the notice was difficult to read` is best classified as:
   A. an observation or finding if supported by the review
   B. a recommendation
   C. a reference number
   D. a subject line
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It describes an observed condition; recommendations follow separately

18. Which phrase is a recommendation?
   A. The office should display a revised checklist.
   B. The notice listed service hours.
   C. Three visitors asked a question.
   D. The review took place on 12 September.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `Should display` proposes an action

19. Which detail is most useful in a reference line?
   A. A prior letter's number and date
   B. A general slogan
   C. An unrelated name
   D. A personal opinion
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The reference identifies earlier correspondence

20. What should an office letter specify when action is time-sensitive?
   A. A reasonable deadline
   B. No timeframe
   C. Only the sender's view
   D. An unrelated date
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A clear deadline supports timely action

21. The model practice offices and dates in this chapter are:
   A. fictional examples
   B. verified government orders
   C. official RPSC templates
   D. legal notifications
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** They are labelled as fictional practice material

22. In an examination task, candidate-supplied names and dates should:
   A. follow the prompt exactly
   B. be replaced with real officials' names
   C. be guessed
   D. be omitted automatically
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Do not alter facts supplied in the question

23. Which statement is appropriate when a complaint has not been verified?
   A. The complaint alleges that the service was delayed.
   B. The officer is proven guilty.
   C. The event definitely occurred.
   D. The report should invent a finding.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It reports the claim as a claim, not as a confirmed conclusion

24. Which format best presents inspection findings and suggestions?
   A. Report
   B. Agenda
   C. Personal invitation
   D. Phrasal-verb list
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** A report organizes method, findings, conclusion, and recommendation

25. Which format is designed to communicate with a named recipient on a specific official matter?
   A. Official letter
   B. Glossary
   C. Minutes
   D. Theme elaboration
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** An official letter addresses a particular recipient and matter

26. Which form is commonly a structured internal office communication or decision note?
   A. Official memorandum
   B. Essay
   C. Idiom
   D. Comprehension passage
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** An official memorandum communicates an office matter in the prescribed form

27. A demi-official letter's tone may be more personal, but it should still:
   A. state the official subject and requested action clearly
   B. omit all dates
   C. make unsupported claims
   D. abandon the record
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Personal tone does not remove the need for clarity

28. What is the best way to stay near a 150-word limit?
   A. Plan a short structure and remove repetition.
   B. Write without checking.
   C. Add a second topic.
   D. Copy a long quotation.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Planning and editing keep the response focused

29. Why should a report distinguish facts from recommendations?
   A. Readers need to know what was observed and what action is proposed.
   B. Both are always identical.
   C. Recommendations are legal findings.
   D. Facts should be omitted.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Clear labels prevent a proposed action from being mistaken for evidence

30. What should a writer do with an unsupported statistic?
   A. Omit it or verify it before use.
   B. Include it for emphasis.
   C. Round it to a larger number.
   D. Attribute it to an unnamed official.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Unsupported data reduces reliability

31. Which sentence is most concise?
   A. The office will review the application.
   B. A review of the application will be undertaken by the office.
   C. The application, which is the subject, will be subject to a review.
   D. The office will make a review that reviews the application.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It expresses the action directly with fewer words

32. The word limit applies to:
   A. the response, as directed by the question
   B. only the address
   C. only the title in every task
   D. nothing in the paper
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Follow the specific instructions about what to include in the count

33. What should a writer check in the final minute?
   A. Names, dates, action, agreement, and format
   B. Only handwriting size
   C. Only the first sentence
   D. Whether a new statistic can be added
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** These checks catch content and language errors

34. Which sentence is appropriately formal?
   A. Please provide the verified statement by 20 September.
   B. Send it whenever you get around to it, okay?
   C. Yo, forward the figures.
   D. Do the thing soon.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It is courteous, clear, and specifies a deadline

35. A report's conclusion should:
   A. follow from the findings
   B. contradict them without explanation
   C. add a new allegation
   D. introduce an unrelated theme
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Conclusions should be evidence-based

36. Which is the clearest request?
   A. Please send the verified list by 20 September.
   B. The list is involved with movement.
   C. Kindly consider all things appropriately.
   D. A list might happen.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It names the action, object, and time

37. What is the role of a distribution/copy list?
   A. Identify offices that receive a copy
   B. Replace the subject
   C. Prove the report's findings
   D. Set the word limit
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It records who else receives the communication

38. What does `in addition` signal?
   A. another related point
   B. a direct contradiction
   C. a condition
   D. a final deadline
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** It adds a related point

39. What is the main risk of overusing formal passive constructions?
   A. The responsible actor may become unclear.
   B. The sentence becomes a question.
   C. The date becomes invalid.
   D. The word count becomes zero.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Passive voice can hide who is expected to act

40. What is the best way to handle a limitation in a report?
   A. State it plainly and keep conclusions within the evidence.
   B. Hide it.
   C. Invent a broader sample.
   D. Delete all findings.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Transparent scope improves trust in the report

41. The current syllabus word count for official writing is approximately:
   A. 50
   B. 100
   C. 150
   D. 300
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** The syllabus indicates approximately 150 words

42. A theme elaboration should:
   A. develop the given theme rather than introduce a separate essay topic
   B. copy the prompt repeatedly
   C. include an unrelated report format
   D. avoid a conclusion
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** The response should remain focused on the assigned theme

43. Which is a useful transition from evidence to a recommendation?
   A. Therefore
   B. For example (in every context)
   C. Although
   D. Meanwhile
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** `Therefore` can introduce a conclusion that follows from evidence

44. A good report's findings should be:
   A. ordered and specific
   B. mixed with unrelated opinion
   C. anonymous and undated in every case
   D. written as a story with invented dialogue
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ordered, specific findings are easier to assess

45. Which is the strongest final review question for a composition task?
   A. Does every sentence help answer the prompt?
   B. Have I added as many idioms as possible?
   C. Is the opening longer than the body?
   D. Did I include a new fact from memory?
   **उत्तर:** A
